RESOLUTION OF SANCTIONING PROCEDURE  
From the procedure instructed by the Spanish Agency for Data Protection and based on the following Background, Proven Facts, and Legal Grounds, the Director of the Spanish Agency for Data Protection resolves to adopt this resolution of the sanctioning procedure.

BACKGROUND  
FIRST: On February 10, 2022, a personal data security breach was reported to the Spanish Agency for Data Protection (AEPD), submitted by ENDESA ENERGÍA, S.A.U. with NIF A81948077 (hereinafter, ENDESA), consisting of a possible unauthorized access to certain commercial systems of Endesa Energía that could have affected about one thousand customers.  
In this notification, the AEPD was informed of the following:  
In August 2021, certain Facebook ads were detected advertising the sale of credentials to access the ***PLATFORM.1 of ENDESA (which contains basic data and data related to the supply point). This incident was assessed, and the relevant measures were adopted. On 01/17/2022, a Facebook ad with similar characteristics to the previous ones was detected, informing about the sale of customer databases for energy and gas. The ad was assessed, and no matches were found with ENDESA customer data included in the CRM system. On 02/08/2022, data from, among others, some ENDESA customers included in the CRM system were detected.  
Approximate number of individuals whose personal data is collected, stored, or processed in any other way; referring exclusively to the processing in which the personal data breach occurred: 6,500,000.  
The incident has been: Intentional, to harm the responsible party / processor or the affected individuals.  
The origin of the incident has been: External: Others, unrelated to the responsible party and processor of the data.  
What may have occurred?: Abuse of access privileges by an employee to extract, forward, or copy personal data.  
As a consequence of the incident, the following has been affected: Confidentiality  
Specifically referring to the data affected by the confidentiality breach.  
Are the data securely encrypted, anonymized, or protected in such a way that they are unintelligible to anyone who may have accessed them or that individuals cannot be identified?: No  
What may have occurred?: Identity theft, Loss of control over their personal data.  
To what extent could the identified consequences affect individuals?:  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
5/214  
Individuals will not be affected or may encounter very limited and reversible inconveniences that they will overcome without any problem (time to re-enter information, discomfort, irritations, etc.)  
As of the date of this notification, are you aware that any of the identified damages have materialized, to the degree indicated in the previous question?: Yes  
Types of affected data: Basic data (e.g., name, surname, date of birth), ID number, foreign ID number, passport and/or any other identifying document, Payment method data (Bank card, etc.), Contact data  
In total, how many individuals have had their data affected by the personal data breach?: 1000  
Indicate the date of detection of the breach: 08/02/2022  
Do you know the date on which the breach began? Approximately 24/08/2021  
Indicate the date on which the breach was resolved: 10/02/2022  
Has the breach been communicated to the affected individuals under the previously described conditions?: They will not be informed  
The affected individuals will not be informed because: There is no high risk to their rights and freedoms  
Along with the notification, Annex I includes the measures taken to resolve the breach and minimize the impact:  
“Following the initial detection in August 2021 of unauthorized sale of access credentials to the platform ***PLATAFORMA.1, the following measures were adopted to mitigate possible negative effects:  
• Resetting the passwords of the affected users to access the platform ***PLATAFORMA.1. This measure is carried out continuously.  
• Monitoring new postings on Facebook. This measure is carried out continuously.  
• Disabling simultaneous sessions on ***PLATAFORMA.1, so that two or more people cannot access at the same time with a single user. This measure was implemented in November 2021.  
• Expanding the traceability (logs) of accesses to ***PLATAFORMA.1 to obtain detailed information on the usage by users. This measure was implemented at the end of December 2021.  
• Requesting the removal of Facebook ads where the sale of such users was offered. This measure is carried out continuously.  
Subsequently, due to new detected events (sale on Facebook of certain databases that contain, among others, customer data from Endesa Energía included in the CRM system that houses basic data, contact data, CUPS, data related to the electricity or gas contract, and account number), the following additional measures will be implemented, among others:

SECOND: With the outgoing record ***REGISTRO.1, the AEPD notifies ENDESA on March 8, 2022, an order to communicate the incident in question to the affected individuals within a maximum period of 30 days in accordance with Article 34 of the GDPR. This order informs that the communication to the interested parties will be made following the provisions of Article 34 of the GDPR, particularly in its second paragraph, requiring a clear and simple description of the nature of the personal data security breach and the possible measures that could be taken by the interested parties based on the risks that, in each case, could be identified by the affected individuals.  
THIRD: On April 6, 2022, this Agency received a document from ENDESA with entry number ***REGISTRO.2, in which it informs that it has proceeded, on April 1, 2022, to send a communication by postal mail to the potentially affected individuals informing them about the events that occurred.  
Along with the document, the following is provided:  
- As Document number 2: the model of the letter sent, in Spanish and Catalan.  
The content of this model in Spanish is as follows:  
“March 31, 2022  
Dear customer,  
We are contacting you to inform you that we have detected a possible unauthorized access to certain commercial systems of Endesa Energía and that from the moment we became aware of this fact, we have adopted the appropriate security measures, both technical and organizational, to prevent a high-risk impact on the rights and freedoms of our customers, ensuring that the confidentiality and integrity of your personal data has not been compromised.  
You can consult our privacy policy at www.endesa.com/es/protec-cion-datos-endesa, where you will find information regarding the processing of your personal data. If you prefer, you can directly contact our Data Protection Officer by sending a communication to ***EMAIL.1 to learn about the details of the measures taken or obtain more information.  
We take this opportunity to thank you for the trust placed in Endesa.  
Best regards,  
Endesa Energía.”  
- As Document number 3: the responsible declaration of the provider ***EMPRESA.2 in which it acknowledges that it printed and made available to the company responsible for postal service 760 shipments on April 1, 2022, and the corresponding delivery receipt to Correos, from the same day.  
FOURTH: On April 20, 2022, the General Subdirectorate of Data Inspection (SGID) received for evaluation the aforementioned notification of the data breach.

the security of personal data submitted by ENDESA, received by this Agency on February 10, 2022, and the General Subdirectorate for Data Inspection (SGID) is ordered to carry out the appropriate preliminary investigations in order to determine a possible violation of data protection regulations.  
FIFTH: The SGID proceeded to carry out preliminary investigative actions to clarify the facts in question, by virtue of the functions assigned to the control authorities in Article 57.1 and the powers granted in Article 58.1 of Regulation (EU) 2016/679 (General Data Protection Regulation, hereinafter GDPR), and in accordance with the provisions established in Title VII, Chapter I, Section Two, of Organic Law 3/2018, of December 5, on Personal Data Protection and the guarantee of digital rights (hereinafter LOPDGDD), being aware of the following points:  
In order to investigate the occurrence of the described facts, on June 27, 2022, via outgoing register ***REGISTRO.3, information was requested from ENDESA and, on September 16, 2022, and November 3, 2022, through outgoing registers ***REGISTRO.4 and ***REGISTRO.5, respectively, further documentation was requested. The responses to these requests were received at the electronic headquarters of the Spanish Agency for Data Protection on July 26, 2022, October 7, 2022, and November 11, 2022, with registration numbers ***REGISTRO.6, ***REGISTRO.7, and ***REGISTRO.8, respectively.  
On September 27, 2022, via outgoing register ***REGISTRO.9, information was requested from the Data Protection Officer of ENDESA (A.A.A.), and on October 19, 2022, with registration number ***REGISTRO.10, his response was received by this Agency.  
Additionally, on October 20, 2022, via outgoing register ***REGISTRO.11, information was requested from ***EMPRESA.1, an external provider of ENDESA, and on November 25, 2022, with registration number ***REGISTRO.12, their response was received at the electronic headquarters of the AEPD.  
In its response to the request, received by this Agency on July 26, 2022, ENDESA stated that:  
1. ***HERRAMIENTA.1 is a tool specifically designed to facilitate advice, attention, and sales in the different commercial channels of Endesa Energía. This tool incorporates the commercial information of the products and services offered by ENDESA at any given time (such as prices, supplements, validity, offers, or conditions), contains technical information of all supply points, as well as certain basic identifying data.  
2. Users of ***HERRAMIENTA.1 can be of two types: (i) “internal” user, enabled for ENDESA employees, and (ii) “external” user, enabled for external providers. There are no other external users, such as customers.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
8/214  
Both internal users and external users have access to all the main functionalities of the tool.  
In turn, from a functional perspective, the platform has the role of "basic" user and the role with "administrator profile." That is, a user with an "administrator profile" can also be either "internal" or "external." Only users who have the "administrator profile" can access the administration menu and upload information into the system (such as new offers, rates, or argumentation). They can also edit certain fields of the tool and obtain extractions and reports.  
3. The registration of external and internal users is carried out by the responsible parties of each Cs or by the support tool's technical assistance, which can be accessed through the Intranet enabled for all internal employees of the Endesa Group. Access requests are made from the Intranet. An explanatory process is attached as Document number 2.  
External and internal users access the platform by entering the username and the associated password.  
4. The platform allows the option to enter the so-called Universal Supply Point Code (hereinafter, "CUPS") or the address of the supply point, to display the associated technical information (described in Document number 4). If the customer already has a valid contract with ENDESA, it also shows the following information: name and surname of the holder of the supply point, their National Identity Document number, contract number, CUPS, annual billing amount, technical data regarding the supply point, consumption data, and usage graph. Within the file related to the supply point, there are several sections that allow recommending other rates to the customer.  
5. The breach was detected on February 8, 2022, when it was confirmed that personal data had been affected in the incident. On February 9, 2022, an assessment of the scope of the breach was made, in which the existence of a risk to the rights and freedoms of the affected individuals was considered, and the need to notify the AEPD was established. The assessment carried out is provided as Document number 5, digitally signed.  
However, the date on which certain possible anomalies were noticed is August 24, 2021, when the person responsible for ***DEPARTMENT.1 of ENDESA sent an email to the Information Security officer of the Endesa Group, informing about a suspicious announcement on Facebook. This notice activated the analysis and investigation process, the first step of which is to confirm the veracity of the announcement. The mentioned email is provided as Document number 6.  
Despite the monitoring of that situation, between both dates, ENDESA did not notice any anomalous situations from which the existence of a security breach could be concluded. Therefore, it was only on February 8, 2022, after the appropriate investigations, that knowledge of the existence of...  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
9/214  
certain coincidences between the customer database of ENDESA and the databases for sale on Facebook, and therefore a new assessment was carried out which resulted in the need to notify the Spanish Agency for Data Protection, an event that occurred on February 10, 2022.  
These investigations were conducted as part of the continuous and systematic monitoring process of "critical events" and, specifically, through the implementation of early detection activities for any risk event carried out within the company. These tasks are performed by ***DEPARTMENT.2, in accordance with the provisions established in ***DOCUMENT.1, which is provided as Document number 7.  
6. Only in the advertisements published on January 17, 2022, by the user B.B.B., was there detected an impact on energy customer databases. Screenshots of the published advertisements are provided as Document number 8.  
7. To determine the number of potentially affected individuals by the incident, the following analysis was conducted:  
(i) The alleged irregular practice carried out by a subcontractor of one of ENDESA's telephone sales service providers was verified, and it was confirmed that it had not extended to other providers.  
(ii) The analysis perimeter was limited to all contracts made between July 1, 2021, when ENDESA's sales volume experienced an unusual and unjustified increase (+500%), and October 15, 2021, when the contract with the provider was terminated, and thus, they ceased to provide their services.  
(iii) Subsequently, a quality control and audit of the contracts subject to assessment was carried out, and those cases in which the customers had already contacted ENDESA to carry out actions related to their contracts (for example, commercial inquiries) were excluded.  
The number of potential individuals affected by the resulting incident was: 760 customers. In any case, once ENDESA communicated the breach to the affected parties, only one email was received from one of the recipients of that communication on April 6, 2022. Documentation verifying their identity was requested, and there has been no response from them. This fact is evidenced by Document number 9.  
8. Although it is not possible to determine the possible objective sought by the individuals facilitating the sale of credentials for access to the platform ***TOOL.1, as well as, if applicable, the sale of ENDESA's customer databases, it is considered that the consequences of the breach are limited to the affected individuals potentially encountering certain inconveniences, very limited and, in any case, reversible.  
Due to the apparently fraudulent objective of data theft, the consequences for the affected customers would fundamentally involve the sale of their data for the purpose of making commercial calls, as well as for sending emails and SMS, offering the possibility of changing energy marketing companies, which, in the worst-case scenarios and if materialized, could result in the change of marketing company without their consent.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
10/214  
9. During the analysis of the incident, three assessments were conducted as information about it was gradually obtained, within the process of constant monitoring that had been carried out since the possible anomalies were detected. The evaluations conducted were as follows:  
On September 13, 2021, the first assessment was made, which included the information obtained up to that moment. Based on this information, it was considered that the derived risk was low, mainly because there was no evidence that fraudulent access to the ***TOOL.1 platform had materialized. The assessment conducted on September 13, 2021, digitally signed, is provided as Document number 10.  
On September 28, 2021, a second assessment was made, which completed the information from the first and concluded that the derived risk continued to be low. The assessment conducted on September 28, 2021, digitally signed, is provided as Document number 11.  
On February 9, 2022, the final assessment was made. It reflected the detection of certain advertisements on Facebook with characteristics similar to the previous ones, in which databases of ENDESA customers appeared. After several analyses of the published databases, matches were detected between the data appearing in those databases and the data that may be included in the commercial system of ENDESA ***TOOL.2). For this reason, it was deemed appropriate to report the incident to the Spanish Data Protection Agency. Reference is made to the assessment conducted on February 9, 2022, digitally signed, previously provided as Document number 5.  
Consequently, and as previously stated, following the new facts detected on February 8, 2022, an assessment of the incident was carried out, taking into account the variation with respect to the previous situation and, proactively, the incident was reported to the Spanish Data Protection Agency on February 10, 2022.  
10. The breach was caused by the apparent fraudulent sale of credentials for the ***TOOL.1 platform. These credentials were assigned to suppliers of Endesa Energía, as external users of the platform, to facilitate tasks related to customer acquisition, advice, and service.  
In this regard, reference is made to Document number 8, previously provided, which includes images of the advertisements for the sale of credentials.  
11. ENDESA became aware of the possible illicit use of the credentials through an email from one of the suppliers indicating irregularities by several of its employees; in that same email, five users were indicated who had been employed in an apparently irregular manner.  
On the other hand, four other users were identified who might be compromised, detected in the demonstration videos and in the screenshots of the published advertisements. The following are the details of all the compromised users:  
• ***USER.1

12. As previously stated, ENDESA has not received any claims from potentially affected customers regarding this incident.

17. In order to reduce the impact of the detected incident and prevent new breaches of this type, additional measures have been adopted, which are detailed below.  
As the incident developed and its possible effects were noticed, it was...  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
12/214  
The following measures have been implemented to reinforce the security of the applications ***HERRAMIENTA.1 and ***HERRAMIENTA.2:  
(i) In the case of ***HERRAMIENTA.2, the multi-factor authentication has been activated in the application, so each user, in addition to entering their credentials in the application access area, must provide a security code received via a previously registered mobile phone. Document number 24 is attached.  
Additionally, the multi-session option has also been disabled. Therefore, each user may only have one active session (Document number 25 is attached).  
(ii) In the case of ***HERRAMIENTA.1, following the incident, the following security measures have been adopted:  
- The multi-factor authentication has been activated in the application (Document number 26 is attached).  
- The multi-session option has been disabled. Therefore, each user may only have one active session (Document number 27 is attached).  
- Additionally, the logs (traceability) collected by the application in the production environment have been improved. This allows for greater evidence of user operations for future analysis and diagnosis. Document number 28 is attached.  
Finally, the last identified announcement related to the illicit use of credentials for the ***HERRAMIENTA.1 platform was on December 13, 2021, and the last identified publication regarding the sale of electricity and gas customer databases occurred in January 2022 (Document number 29 is attached). Consequently, no new publications related to this incident have been detected after the notification made on February 10, 2022.  
18. Document number 30 is provided, which is a copy of the incident log where the assessments made during the years 2021 and 2022 related to this case appear.  
19. On September 15, 2022, the SGID verified that advertisements identified by ENDESA offering the sale of access credentials to ***HERRAMIENTA.1 and databases were still published on Facebook (see the diligence document dated December 16, 2022, in the file).  
In its response to the request, received by this Agency on October 7, 2022, ENDESA stated that:  
20. ENDESA identified in the advertisements and demonstration videos published on the social network Facebook four users of the tool ***HERRAMIENTA.1 (hereinafter “***HERRAMIENTA.1”) who were therefore compromised.

Additionally, the other five compromised users of ***HERRAMIENTA.1 were identified by the subcontractor company of an ENDESA provider, which alerted about the irregular use that some of its advisors had made with certain access credentials to ***HERRAMIENTA.1 that they had acquired illegally (Document number 3 is attached).

22. The ENDESA supplier that provided telephone sales services, whose subcontractor carried out the alleged irregular practice, was ***EMPRESA.1, and the subcontractor company to which it entrusted part of the commissioned services was ***EMPRESA.9.  
Document number 4 is provided, which is a copy of the contract signed between ENDESA and ***EMPRESA.1.  
Regarding the subcontracting of ***EMPRESA.9, Document number 5 is provided, which is an excerpt of the general contracting conditions applicable to the contract signed between ENDESA and ***EMPRESA.1 concerning the subcontracting regime, and Document number 6 is provided, which is the authorization issued by ENDESA to carry out the aforementioned subcontracting of ***EMPRESA.9.  
23. Since the detection of the advertisements for the illegal sale of access credentials to the tool ***HERRAMIENTA.1 on the social network Facebook, as a first measure, the removal of the advertisements was repeatedly requested through the standard form enabled for this purpose by Facebook (as evidenced by Document number 7 as an example). As a result, Facebook disabled access or removed the content of the advertisements, but not all of them. Document number 8 is provided, which includes some of the responses provided by Facebook in this regard.  
Secondly, in order to expedite the removal of all detected advertisements, on February 4, 2022, a written communication was sent via burofax to FACEBOOK SPAIN S.L. (hereinafter “FB SPAIN”), which is provided as Document number 9, informing them of the publication by users of the platform of certain information that could constitute a crime and requesting their collaboration for its removal. On February 8, 2022, this communication was responded to via email, indicating that FB SPAIN was not the competent entity to resolve the matter.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
14/214  
issue, but rather FACEBOOK IRELAND LIMITED, and requesting that the communication be directed to said entity (Document number 10 is provided). Finally, on February 28, 2022, ENDESA sent a new communication (Document number 11 is provided), reiterating its initial letter to FB SPAIN.  
24. In the analysis carried out for the correct identification of the clients whose data had apparently been unlawfully accessed from the ***HERRAMIENTA.3 tool and, therefore, could potentially have been processed without their consent, the following actions were taken:  
i. First, once the provider ***EMPRESA.1 informed ENDESA of the irregular practice committed by personnel from ***EMPRESA.9 and the appropriate verifications were conducted, it was confirmed that this practice did not extend to other providers.  
ii. Second, once the possible origin of the allegedly irregular contracts was identified, the temporal scope of their actions was limited, verifying that from July 1, 2021, the sales volume of ENDESA experienced an unusual and unjustified increase (specifically, sales increased by more than 500%), which could be representative of the irregular actions of the personnel hired by ***EMPRESA.9. As of October 15, 2021, the activity of this subcontractor was ceased – as a result of the termination of the contract with ***EMPRESA.9 – thus limiting the temporal analysis of the contracts to the period between July 1 and October 15, 2015.  
iii. Finally, a quality control and audit of the contracts carried out by ***EMPRESA.9 within the established temporal scope was conducted. In this analysis, cases were excluded where the clients on whose behalf a contract was made involving ***EMPRESA.9 – within the defined time period – had contacted ENDESA to carry out actions related to their contracts (for example, commercial inquiries), which indicated that the clients themselves were aware of their contracts and did not express any objections.  
25. Although it was on February 8, 2022, when, after the appropriate investigations, the extent of the security breach was understood upon confirming the existence of matches between ENDESA's customer database and the databases for sale on Facebook, ENDESA, on January 31, 2022, due to the breach of established procedures, decided to terminate the contract with ***EMPRESA.1 for violating the commitments made to Endesa Energía by said contractor.  
Furthermore, the employees of ***EMPRESA.9 who allegedly made irregular use of access credentials to ***HERRAMIENTA.1 were dismissed as communicated by the company itself in the email dated October 20, 2021, and reported to local authorities (Document number 12 is provided).  
In its response to the request, received by this Agency on October 19, 2022, the DPD of ENDESA stated that:

26. The role of the Data Protection Officer is regulated in accordance with the provisions established in ***DOCUMENTO.2 (currently undergoing its fourth review). According to this internal regulation and other Group regulations, it is the responsibility of the Data Protection Officer to provide advice on Personal Data Protection, cooperating with other areas of the organization on matters that may relate -among others- to security incidents. This is a task carried out continuously, as coordination with the affected areas of the organization is permanent and operates through various mechanisms such as periodic meetings, issuance of reports, or participation in committees.  
27. As part of this ongoing advice, as stated in the information included in the reports corresponding to the incident assessments conducted on September 13 and 28, 2021, and February 9, 2022, in his capacity as Data Protection Officer and together with the Security area, the Systems area, the ENDESA Sales team, and ***DEPARTAMENTO.3 participated in the various evaluations carried out in relation to the Breach. It should be noted that in the reports corresponding to the assessments conducted on September 13 and 28, 2021, it was highlighted that “…from ***DEPARTAMENTO.4, the need to continue the investigation is indicated in order to carry out a new assessment of the incident, as it is considered that the information provided is not complete and the present analysis is preliminary.” Finally, in the report corresponding to the assessment on February 9, 2022, it was stated that “…after conducting an assessment, in a timely manner, of the scope of the security incident and the possible repercussions for the rights and freedoms of the affected data subjects, the preliminary decision is made to communicate the security incident to the supervisory authority.” Therefore, as a consequence of the advice provided by the Data Protection Officer, the Breach was communicated to the Spanish Data Protection Agency on February 10, 2022.  
28. The Data Protection Officer sent an email on February 9, 2022, in which he insisted on the need to carry out the notification of the Breach (attached as Document number 1). As a result of this request, the communication was made the following day after fully gathering the information requested in the notification form of the Spanish Data Protection Agency.  
29. As part of the ongoing advice, as Data Protection Officer, personally and through the team of the Data Protection Office that he directs, constant monitoring of the Breach was carried out, particularly to determine the appropriateness or not of communication in light of any developments that may arise or the criteria that may emanate from that Agency.

32. During the investigation initiated at the end of August 2021, a total of 440 advertisements were identified on the social network Facebook offering the rental and/or sale of access credentials to the ENDESA ***TOOL.1.  
The two users identified from whose Facebook profiles these advertisements were published were those corresponding to "B.B.B." (who presumably also made posts under the profile of "C.C.C.") and "D.D.D.". During the course of the investigation, the Information Security area of ENDESA made contact with user B.B.B., who offered the sale of credentials to access ***TOOL.1 in exchange for a monthly payment. They also indicated that they had different users available for sale.  
Thanks to the continuous and systematic process of monitoring "critical events" at ENDESA, new advertisements published by user B.B.B. were identified during January 2022, where the sale of customer databases from various energy supply companies was offered. After the appropriate investigations, in February 2022, certain matches between that database and the customer database of ENDESA housed in application ***TOOL.2 were verified – which led to an assessment that resulted in the need to notify the Spanish Agency for Data Protection, an event that occurred on February 10, 2022.

As a result of conversations held between the managers of ENDESA and their suppliers, and subsequently between these and their subcontractors, it has come to our attention that behind the user “B.B.B.” / “C.C.C.” is C.C.C., who, as has been communicated to this Office, was employed until the year 2016 by ***EMPRESA.10. This branch has never provided services for ENDESA, and consequently, C.C.C. never had an access user assigned to his name for ENDESA applications (Document number 3 is provided).  
On the other hand, the user “D.D.D.” corresponds to the employee of ***EMPRESA.11, a subcontracting company in ***PAÍS.1 of the supplier ***EMPRESA.12, who provides services for ENDESA, D.D.D., who, however, resigned at the beginning of the year 2020 (Document number 4 is provided).

Consequently, the two advertisers selling ENDESA user credentials on Facebook were not former employees of suppliers (or subcontractors of these) of ENDESA at the time of the ad publication (one was never an employee, and the other stopped working for a subcontractor of an ENDESA supplier long before the publication of the ads that led to the Breach).

36. The identified users had a role of "user" in the tool ***TOOL.1, with assigned permission for consultation, which allowed them to only view (i) in relation to all consumers, customers or not of ENDESA, technical data – such as power, voltage, estimated consumption – associated with the supply point by entering the address or the so-called Universal Supply Point Code (hereinafter "CUPS"); and (ii) in the case of ENDESA customers with a valid contract, they could access the following information: name and surname of the holder of the supply point, National Identity Document number, contract number, CUPS, annual billing amount, debt indicator, pricing information, technical data regarding the supply point, consumption data, and usage graph.  
The approximate volume of supply points that can potentially be consulted – one by one, individually – from the tool ***TOOL.1, provided that a CUPS or an address is entered beforehand, is 30.6 million electricity supply points and 8.6 million gas supply points. In the case of customers with a valid contract, the approximate volume that a user can consult, under the same premises indicated above, is 4.8 million electricity customers and 1.2 million gas customers.  
Regarding the tool ***TOOL.2, the identified committed users could access data related to customers with a valid contract with ENDESA, with the approximate volume during the period from July 1, 2021, to October 15, 2021, being 4.8 million electricity customers and 1.2 million gas customers. As for the type of data that could be viewed, they mainly include: name and surname of the holder of the supply point, postal address, National Identity Document number, CUPS, phone number, email, contracted products, invoices, bank account number, credit quality, debts with the company.  
Finally, as has been communicated to this Office, regarding the committed users and the period in which the Breach occurred, there is no record (logs) of the use of the tools ***TOOL.1 or ***TOOL.2. It is necessary to indicate that, due to the Breach, a series of measures were adopted, including the expansion of traceability (logs) of access to ***TOOL.1 to obtain detailed information on the actual use made by users – a measure that was implemented at the end of December 2021.  
37. Document number 11 is provided, a copy of the burofax sent to ***COMPANY.1 notifying the termination of the contract, which has been communicated to this Office by the corresponding unit responsible for the actions taken in this regard.  
In its response to the request, received by this Agency on November 25, 2022, ***COMPANY.1 has stated that:  
38. Regarding the irregular practice carried out by personnel from the subcontracted company ***COMPANY.9 (***COMPANY.9), a detailed and chronological description of the events:  
- 19/10/2021. ***COMPANY.1 detects that a user from the agent ***COMPANY.9 has attempted to carry out a fraudulent registration since, among other issues, it can be seen that the phone number used to send the customer the SMS confirmation of sale (the customer must respond to this SMS to confirm the sale) is the same as another phone number used for the same purpose for another customer.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
21/214  
This means that it is possible that the client did not accept the contract, having accepted it through SMS confirmation by a person other than the account holder.  
- 19/10/2021. Upon suspicion that this may have been carried out by other agents of ***EMPRESA.9, ***EMPRESA.1 initiates an internal audit and communicates to the person responsible for their account at ENDESA what happened via telephone, also sending an email (the email is attached as image 1 in Document 2) with the initial inquiries made and the first measures taken in this regard. On the same day, a total closure of all users of ***EMPRESA.9 and access to any tool of the ENDESA campaign is carried out, and an internal audit is initiated to assess the volume of clients affected by fraudulent contracts.  
- 19/10/2021. ***EMPRESA.1 sends a communication to all its Subagents (***EMPRESA.9 and other collaborators) participating in the ENDESA campaign informing them of what happened, the internal audit opened, and the consequences of engaging in such criminal practices (the email is attached as image 2 in Document 2).  
- 20/10/2021. ***EMPRESA.1, after the audit conducted, sends a communication to ENDESA indicating the operations affected by the fraudulent practice of ***EMPRESA.9, identifying 137 contracts made and another 7 operations pending validation by ENDESA that have not been registered. Additionally, 172 orders pending validation by the back office of ***EMPRESA.9 are identified and blocked to prevent their processing. Furthermore, this communication outlines the measures intended to be implemented in the coming days to detect any other additional irregularities.  
Among these measures, it is proposed to ENDESA to make calls to clients who may have been affected to confirm both the legality of the contract and the verification of the account number provided. However, ENDESA prohibits ***EMPRESA.1 from making these commercial calls indicating that they will proceed themselves to verify the contracts (the communication sent to Endesa is attached as image 3 in Document 2).  
On the other hand, in this communication made to ENDESA, an email from the manager of ***EMPRESA.9 is attached, informing ***EMPRESA.1 of the complaints filed with the Police of ***PAÍS.1 against its agents involved in this fraudulent practice, as well as the phone numbers used fraudulently for the confirmation of sales via SMS.  
- 25/10/2021. To prevent workers from ***EMPRESA.9 from seeking refuge in other subcontractors, on October 25, ***EMPRESA.1 proceeded to close the systems and to officially communicate to the rest of the agents in the area the closure of the campaign.  
- 27/10/2021. ***EMPRESA.1 receives an email from the manager of ***EMPRESA.9 detailing the inquiries made regarding the fraudulent practice carried out by some of its agents and their modus operandi, as some of the salespeople have decided to cooperate after seeing the complaint filed against them with the Police. This email details the three ways of operating, according to the management of ***EMPRESA.9, of the salespeople.  
The operating methods detailed in the email are as follows:  
1. They contacted the client and explained the product; if the client agreed and was aware of the contract, they proceeded with the closing. However, the client raised an objection indicating that they could not respond to the SMS for various reasons (blocked for SMS, did not have a mobile phone, no battery, about to travel, etc.). What the salesperson did was obtain a number from another client who was with Endesa and asked this latter to respond to the SMS.  
2. Through the internet, the salespeople contracted the ***PLATAFORMA.1 program from ENDESA. As can be seen in images 4.1, 4.2, 4.3, and 4.4 attached in Document 2, different people "marketed" access to the ***PLATAFORMA.1 program from ENDESA. What the salespeople did was use this tool to extract data from ENDESA clients, so the recordings were made with other people.  
3. Another case involved clients who, at some point, did not want to pass texts because they were too long and got tired of receiving several calls. In this case, they simulated sales with other people, and the message was also responded to by others (the email sent by ***EMPRESA.9 and received by ***EMPRESA.1 is attached as image 4 in Document 2).  
39. The evaluation report of the breach presented at the internal risk committee is attached as Document 1. It should be noted that the breach is managed in the capacity of Data Processor, having collaborated with the responsible party to the extent that they requested and allowed such collaboration.

42. In order to understand the relationship between the parties involved in the contracting of products and services from ENDESA, it is important to clarify that:  
• Each user of ***COMPANY.9 had an independent user in the CRM of ***COMPANY.1 to perform the upload of the requests for contracts that they had formalized through the commercial solicitation calls made. Through their user, each agent uploaded the following information into the CRM of ***COMPANY.1:  
- The necessary data for the client to carry out the contracting.  
- The recording of the call from start to finish, including the commercial offer + legal texts + client consent to the commercial offer and contracting of the Offered Products and Services, and the necessary files for the complete call submission.  
Once this information was uploaded by each agent, an Excel file was generated in the systems of ***COMPANY.1 with the necessary data for the Back Office of ENDESA to manage the data in the commercial systems of ENDESA. The data used to generate the Excel was obtained from the upload that the agent had previously made in the CRM of ***COMPANY.1.  
Additionally, a certificate was generated by a trusted third party with the SMS sent for the confirmation of the sale, which also certified the YES or NO from the client regarding the Commercial Offer.  
• Once the above procedure was completed, ***COMPANY.1 proceeded, daily, to perform a bulk upload via FTP in the tool ***TOOL.4, owned by ENDESA, of all the information described above, that is, the recording of the call, the certificate from a trusted third party of the acceptance SMS from the client, and the Excel with the client information for management by the Back Office of ENDESA. For this bulk upload, ***COMPANY.1 only had access to the user of ***TOOL.4 and FTP access.  
In relation to the aforementioned, it is important to highlight that the sub-agents (users of ***COMPANY.9) could only access the CRM of ***COMPANY.1, without having any direct access to the CRM of Endesa.  
43. Regarding the users of ***COMPANY.9 with access to the CRM of ***COMPANY.1, it is noteworthy that there are different user profiles in the CRM of ***COMPANY.1 (Doc. 4, image 1 is attached to certify the relationship of user profiles), with ***COMPANY.1 being responsible for the application in any case.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid

24/214  
44. For its part, ***EMPRESA.1 only had one user with access to the following ENDESA tools:  
- ***HERRAMIENTA.4 and the FTP access authorized by ENDESA to perform the bulk upload of contracting requests. (Doc. 4, images 4 and 5 are attached to substantiate the registration process in the application). With the user profile authorized by ENDESA, it was only possible to upload sales from the CRM of ***EMPRESA.1 to ***HERRAMIENTA.4 and view ENDESA's feedback on the status of the uploaded sales. (…)  
- ***PLATAFORMA.1: This is the platform for downloading ENDESA's "do not disturb" files. ***EMPRESA.1, through this platform, downloaded the files that ENDESA made available to it weekly, which contained the phone numbers of customers who had exercised their right to object to receiving commercial calls from ENDESA. Once this file was obtained, it was cross-referenced with the databases used for making commercial calls. There was only one user with access to this application, and access was through the web. ***EMPRESA.1 only had permissions in this application to download the file. There is no other application that ***EMPRESA.1 and/or its collaborators had access to within the framework of the contractual relationship with ENDESA for the telephone recruitment of customers.  
45. Each user of ***EMPRESA.9 could upload to the CRM of ***EMPRESA.1 exclusively the operations that they themselves had carried out, being able to view only the statuses of those operations until the complete closure of the sale by ENDESA. They only had access to the personal data that they themselves entered into the CRM of ***EMPRESA.1, which were, in any case, identifying and contact data (name, surname, ID number, phone number, email, supply point address, voice, CUPS number, billing address), economic data (bank account number), and personal characteristics (date of birth). A table is provided differentiating between customers with whom the complete contracting process was finalized and customers with whom, for various reasons, the contracting was not formalized. In any case, not all customers to whom these users had access were affected by the fraudulent practice of ***EMPRESA.9, as only 137 operations were affected whose fraudulent contracting was completed. For its part, ***EMPRESA.1 could access ***HERRAMIENTA.4 with its user to view the status of any contracting by any of its Subagents, being able to access the same type of personal data as described in the previous point.  
46. In the CRM of ***EMPRESA.1, the following were recorded:  
- the access logs of users to the CRM of ***EMPRESA.1 (date, time, IP address, and result)  
- the logs of access registrations to the server URLs (date, time, IP address, and result)  
- Traceability in the database (date, time, and user) of the creation of an operation by a user and of a modification of the status of the operation.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
25/214  
operation (for example, if there is any incident in the contracting, lack of documentation, or the contracting has been denied by ENDESA).  
Additionally, it was allowed to impose an access limitation to the CRM by IP so that users could only access from an IP known to a higher user profile. It is noteworthy that the fraudulent practices carried out by the users of ***EMPRESA.9 were due to intentional actions performed by the users themselves, that is, the contracts were not executed due to unauthorized access by third parties to the CRM of ***EMPRESA.1, but rather through premeditated actions by the authorized users. For this reason, in the log analysis conducted, a "normal" operation of user activity is observed, without any irregularities in the use of the users being noticeable.  
47. After the internal audit conducted by ***EMPRESA.1 upon becoming aware of the fraudulent practice carried out by ***EMPRESA.9, it was determined that there were a total of 137 contracts that were possibly affected. This was deduced because, in these operations, the phone number used for sending the SMS requesting customer consent to close the sale matched that of at least one other operation.  
48. Attached as Document 5 is a copy of the contract signed with ***EMPRESA.9 that was in force during the period when the security breach occurred, as well as its termination effective October 19, 2021, the day on which the fraudulent practice carried out by ***EMPRESA.9 was discovered. In the contract termination document, a typographical error can be seen in the date of the contract subject to termination, as it refers to the date on which the first contract with ***EMPRESA.9 for the distribution of ENDESA services was formalized, which was no longer in force on the date of the security breach. In any case, since it was the only commercial campaign that ***EMPRESA.9 managed on behalf of ***EMPRESA.1 in favor of ENDESA, with the aforementioned termination communication, the commercial relationship between the parties was understood to be resolved, and among other things, access for their users to load sales in the CRM of ***EMPRESA.1 was withdrawn.  
According to the diligence carried out on October 13, 2022, ENDESA is a public limited company of Spanish nationality. According to the data available in AXESOR, the company has a "Large" size with XXX employees, and in the year 2021 (the last fiscal year presented), it had a sales volume of ***CANTIDAD.1 euros and an operating result of ***CANTIDAD.3 euros.  
SIXTH: On January 27, 2023, the Director of the Spanish Agency for Data Protection agreed to initiate sanctioning proceedings against ENDESA in accordance with the provisions of Articles 63 and 64 of the LPACAP, in which it was indicated that it had a period of ten days to present allegations, in order to impose:  
- For the alleged infringement of Article 5.1.f) of the GDPR, classified in Article 83.5 of said regulation, an administrative fine of 2,500,000.00 euros.  
- For the alleged infringement of Article 32 of the GDPR, classified in Article 83.4 of said regulation, an administrative fine of 1,500,000.00 euros.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
26/214  
- For the alleged violation of Article 33 of the GDPR, classified under Article 83.4 of said regulation, an administrative fine of 800,000.00 euros.  
- For the alleged violation of Article 34 of the GDPR, classified under Article 83.4 of said regulation, an administrative fine of 800,000.00 euros.  
- For the alleged violation of Article 44 of the GDPR, classified under Article 83.5 of said regulation, an administrative fine of 2,000,000.00 euros.  
This initiation agreement, which was notified to ENDESA in accordance with the provisions established in Law 39/2015, of October 1, on the Common Administrative Procedure of Public Administrations (LPACAP), was received on January 30, 2023.  
SEVENTH: On January 30 and 31, and February 1, 2, and 3, 2023, ENDESA submitted a document requesting an extension of the deadline to present allegations and to be provided with a copy of the file.  
On February 3, 2023, the instructing body of the procedure agreed to extend the requested deadline by a maximum of five days, in accordance with the provisions of Article 32.1 of the LPACAP, and to send ENDESA a copy of the file.  
The aforementioned agreement was notified to ENDESA on February 6, 2023, as evidenced by the acknowledgment of receipt in the file.  
EIGHTH: On February 20, 2023, a timely and proper document from ENDESA was received by this Agency, in which it presented allegations against the initiation agreement.  
In these allegations, in summary, it stated that:  
- Preliminary allegation: Specification of certain essential factual points for the legal analysis of the file, since there are, in its view, some issues in the Initiation Agreement that, being very relevant for the assessment of ENDESA's conduct, have not been interpreted by the AEPD, in its opinion, correctly.  
- ENDESA had implemented security measures appropriate to the risk:  
  - Document number 1 is provided with a detailed explanation of each of the developments in the platform ***HERRAMIENTA.1, as well as its hourly valuation.  
  - Document number 2 is provided with a detailed explanation of each of the developments and tasks necessary for the implementation of the security measure in ***HERRAMIENTA.2.  
- There is concurrence of violations of Article 5.1.f) of the GDPR and Article 32 of the GDPR.

THIRTIETH: On November 2, 2021, ***COMPANY.9 was deactivated as a supplier in ENDESA's systems, as stated in Document 3 of the writing submitted by ENDESA on July 3, 2023.  
THIRTY-FIRST: On November 4, 2021, as stated in Document 1 of the writing from ***COMPANY.1 dated November 25, 2022, ***COMPANY.1 prepared a report on the violation of personal data security in question, detected by them on October 19, 2021. The report indicates:  
“(...)  
The security breach can be classified as an integrity breach insofar as there has been an intentional alteration of the mobile phone number of the client used to send the SMS confirmation of the sale, with the aim of having another person accept the sale, and, in some cases, there has also been an intentional alteration of the client's bank account number.  
Likewise, the security breach can be considered a breach of confidentiality since, in some cases, the call recording was made with a fictitious client, and therefore, this person had access to personal data of the client whose identity was being impersonated.  
(...)  
Volume of affected individuals: There are 137 sales operations affected by completed fraudulent contracting, another 7 sales operations pending validation by Endesa, and 172 sales operations that were blocked while pending validation by the back office of ***COMPANY.9. There may be individuals who have been affected by two or more fraudulent contracts, so the number of affected individuals will be less than the number of sales operations indicated. The final number of affected individuals will be determined by Endesa upon completion of the management.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
40/214  
to close the security breach as they do not allow ***COMPANY.1 to continue collaborating with them in resolving the breach.  
However, the affected individuals are easily identifiable.  
Profile of affected individuals: Adults aged 18 to 65 residing in Spain, clients or potential clients of Endesa.  
The severity level of the consequences for the affected individuals can be considered high as they could face energy supply cuts, stress, and additional costs.  
(...)  
The security breach suffered is due to the commission of a crime of identity theft and fraudulent contracting by the company ***COMPANY.9, which may have negative consequences for the affected parties.  
After the analysis conducted, it is concluded that the verification procedure for sales made must be reinforced, as well as the procedure imposed by Endesa on its distributors for telephone contracting. With the currently imposed procedure, there are cases where it is difficult to verify whether the identity of the person claiming to contract the service corresponds to the actual person contracting it.  
For example, in a real situation, a real client could request that a different phone number be entered for contracting purposes than the number from which the commercial call is being made, or they could even request that the SMS confirmation of the sale be sent to a different number than the one where the commercial call is being made (for example, if the call is being received on a landline).  
On the other hand, if the operation were subject to fraud, as has been the case, even with a welcome or loyalty call, there would be a possibility that the fraud would not be identified since the involved salesperson could have entered a phone number different from the actual one of the contract holder.  
Therefore, we understand that additional measures are needed from Endesa to identify the contract holders, as it is not possible to expand technical measures to limit security breaches like the one suffered, considering the procedure that ***COMPANY.1, as Data Processor, must follow at the request of the Controller.  
Currently, the security breach is still being managed by Endesa.

FIFTY-FIFTH: According to ENDESA in its letter submitted on July 26, 2022, ***HERRAMIENTA.1 is a tool specially designed to facilitate advice, attention, and sales in the different commercial channels of ENDESA.  
This tool incorporates commercial information about the products and services offered by ENDESA at any given time (such as prices, supplements, validity, offers, or conditions), contains technical information about all supply points, as well as certain basic identifying data.  
FIFTY-SIXTH: According to ENDESA in its letter submitted on July 26, 2022, users of ***HERRAMIENTA.1 can be of two types: (i) “internal” user, enabled for employees of Endesa Energía, and (ii) “external” user, enabled for external suppliers. There are no other external users, such as customers.  
Both internal users and external users have access to all the main functionalities of the tool.  
Furthermore, from a functional perspective, the platform has the role of “basic” user and the role with “administrator profile.” That is, a user with an “administrator profile” can also be either “internal” or “external.” Only users with the “administrator profile” can access the administration menu and upload information into the system (such as new offers, rates, or scripts). They can also edit certain fields of the tool and obtain extractions and reports.  
FIFTY-SEVENTH: According to ENDESA in its letter submitted on July 26, 2022, the registration of external and internal users is carried out by the managers of each sales or support channel in the corporate technical support tool accessed through the Intranet available to all internal employees of the Endesa Group. Access requests are made from the Intranet.  
External and internal users access the platform by entering their username and associated password.  
FIFTY-EIGHTH: According to ENDESA in its letter submitted on July 26, 2022, the platform allows the option to enter the so-called Universal Supply Point Code (hereinafter, “CUPS”) or the address of the supply point, to display the associated technical information (described in Document number 4). If the customer already has an active contract with Endesa Energía, it also shows the following information: name and surname of the holder of the supply point, their National Identity Document number, contract number, CUPS, annual billing amount, technical data regarding the supply point, consumption data, and usage graph. Within the file related to the supply point, there are several sections that allow recommending other rates to the customer.  
FIFTY-NINTH: According to ENDESA in its letter submitted on July 26, 2022, to determine the number of potentially affected individuals by the incident, the following analysis was carried out:  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
52/214  
(i) It was verified that the alleged irregular practice carried out by a subcontractor of one of the suppliers of ENDESA, which provides telephone sales services, had not extended to other suppliers.  
(ii) The analysis perimeter was limited to all contracts made between July 1, 2021, the date on which ENDESA's sales volume experienced an unusual and unjustified increase (+500%), and October 15, 2021, the date on which the contract with the supplier was terminated, and thus, it ceased to provide its services.  
(iii) Subsequently, a quality control and audit of the contracts under evaluation was carried out, and those cases in which the clients themselves had already contacted ENDESA to perform actions related to their contracts (for example, commercial inquiries) were discarded.  

SIXTIETH: According to ENDESA's statement presented on July 26, 2022, the number of potential individuals affected by the resulting incident was: 760 clients.  
SIXTY-FIRST: According to ENDESA's statement presented on July 26, 2022, although it was not possible to determine the possible objective sought by the individuals who facilitated the sale of credentials for access to the platform ***HERRAMIENTA.1, as well as, where applicable, the sale of customer databases of Endesa Energía, ENDESA considers that the consequences of the violation of personal data security are limited to the fact that the affected individuals could encounter certain inconveniences, very limited and, in any case, reversible. Due to the apparently fraudulent objective of data theft, the consequences for the affected clients would fundamentally be the sale of their data for the purpose of making commercial calls, as well as for sending emails and SMS, offering the possibility of changing energy marketing companies, which, in the worst-case scenarios and if it were to materialize, could result in the change of marketing company without their consent.  
SIXTY-SECOND: According to ENDESA's statement presented on July 26, 2022, the violation of personal data security was caused by the apparent fraudulent sale of credentials for the platform ***HERRAMIENTA.1. These credentials were assigned to ENDESA suppliers, as external users of the platform, to facilitate tasks related to customer acquisition, advising, and customer service.  
SIXTY-THIRD: According to ENDESA's statement presented on July 26, 2022, ENDESA became aware of the possible illicit use of the credentials through an email from one of the suppliers indicating irregularities by several of their employees; in that same email, five users were identified who had been employed in an apparently irregular manner.

EIGHTY-FOURTH: As stated by ENDESA in its letter dated November 11, 2022, "the committed users had a 'user' role in the tool ***TOOL.1, with assigned permission for consultation, so they could only view (i) regarding all consumers, whether customers of Endesa Energía or not, technical data – such as power, voltage, estimated consumption – associated with the supply point by entering the address or the so-called Universal Supply Point Code (hereinafter 'CUPS'); and (ii) if they were customers of Endesa Energía with a valid contract, they could access the following information: name and surname of the holder of the supply point, National Identity Document number, contract number, CUPS, annual billing amount, debt indicator, information on prices, technical data related to the supply point, consumption data, and usage graph.  
The approximate volume of supply points that can potentially be consulted – one by one, individually – from the tool ***TOOL.1, provided that a CUPS or an address is previously entered, is 30.6 million electricity supply points and 8.6 million gas supply points. In the case of customers with a valid contract, the approximate volume that a user can consult, under the same premises indicated above, is 4.8 million electricity customers and 1.2 million gas customers.  
Regarding the tool ***TOOL.2, the identified committed users could access data related to customers with a valid contract with ENDESA, with the approximate volume during the period from July 1, 2021, to October 15, 2021, being 4.8 million electricity customers and 1.2 million gas customers. As for the type of data that could be viewed, it mainly includes: name and surname of the holder of the supply point, postal address, National Identity Document number, CUPS, phone number, email, contracted products, invoices, bank account number, credit quality, debts with the company.  
Regarding the committed users and the period during which the personal data security breach occurred, there is no record (logs) of the use of the tools ***TOOL.1 or ***TOOL.2.  
As a result of the personal data security breach, a series of measures were adopted, including the expansion of traceability (logs) of accesses to ***TOOL.1 to obtain detailed information on the actual use made by users – a measure that was implemented at the end of December 2021."

LEGAL GROUNDS  
I  
Competence  
In accordance with the powers granted to each supervisory authority by Article 58.2 of Regulation (EU) 2016/679 (General Data Protection Regulation, hereinafter GDPR), and as established in Articles 47, 48.1, 64.2, and 68.1 of Organic Law 3/2018, of December 5, on the Protection of Personal Data and Guarantee of Digital Rights (hereinafter, LOPDGDD), the Director of the Spanish Data Protection Agency is competent to initiate and resolve this procedure.  
Furthermore, Article 63.2 of the LOPDGDD states: “The procedures processed by the Spanish Data Protection Agency shall be governed by the provisions of Regulation (EU) 2016/679, by this organic law, by the regulatory provisions issued in its development, and, in so far as they do not contradict them, subsidiarily by the general rules on administrative procedures.”  
II  
Preliminary Issues  
In this case, in accordance with the provisions of Article 4.1 of the GDPR, there is evidence of the processing of personal data, given that ENDESA carries out, among other processes, the collection and storage of the following personal data of individuals, such as: first name and surname, identity document, address, telephone number, banking data, supply data, among others.  
ENDESA carries out this activity in its capacity as data controller, as it is the entity that determines the purposes and means of such activity, pursuant to Article 4.7 of the GDPR.  
Article 4, paragraph 12 of the GDPR broadly defines a “personal data breach” as “a breach of security that results in the accidental or unlawful destruction, loss, alteration of, or unauthorized access to personal data transmitted, stored, or otherwise processed.”  
In this case, there is a personal data breach under the circumstances indicated above, categorized as a confidentiality breach, due to unauthorized access to personal data processed by ENDESA, and as an integrity breach, due to the modification of certain data to carry out fraudulent registrations.  
Among the principles of processing provided for in Article 5 of the GDPR, the integrity and confidentiality of personal data are guaranteed in paragraph 1.f) of Article 5 of the GDPR. The security of personal data is regulated in Articles 32, 33, and 34 of the GDPR, which govern the security of processing, the notification of a personal data breach to the supervisory authority, and the communication to the data subject, respectively.  
Finally, international transfers of personal data are regulated in Articles 44 and following of the GDPR.  
III  
Claims Made  
Claims regarding the initiation of the sanctioning procedure  
In relation to the claims made regarding the initiation of the current sanctioning procedure, responses are provided in the order presented by ENDESA:  
Preliminary.- Specification of certain essential factual points for the legal analysis of the file  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
63/214  
ENDESA claims the existence, in the Initiation Agreement, of certain issues that, while being very relevant for the assessment of Endesa's conduct, have not been interpreted correctly by the AEPD:  
(a) Evidentiary value of the security breach assessment and management report prepared by ***EMPRESA.1:  
ENDESA argues that the AEPD bases much of its reasoning on the security breach assessment and management report prepared by ***EMPRESA.1 on November 4, 2021.  
And that: (i) ***EMPRESA.1 never shared said report, nor its conclusions, with Endesa (even when Endesa continued to request information about the case from ***EMPRESA.1), despite having a contractual and legal obligation to inform Endesa of all details of the incident of which it was aware; and (ii) when ***EMPRESA.1 provided the report to the AEPD (November 2022), Endesa had already terminated the service provision contract with ***DEPARTAMENTO.6 of Endesa with this provider (January 2022), and furthermore, the relationship between that channel and ***EMPRESA.1 was not "friendly."  
ENDESA claims that, since the date of its preparation (November 4, 2021), it was never updated by ***EMPRESA.1 with the new information that was emerging from Endesa's investigations and that Endesa shared with ***EMPRESA.1, which means it does not reflect the reality of the investigations into the data security breach or its actual consequences. It is for this lack of rigor that Endesa also believes that this report should be excluded from the evidentiary list of the File and, therefore, its content should not be considered as a basis for making any decision within the framework of this File.  
ENDESA emphasizes that the report provided by ***EMPRESA.1 should be considered a partial report (in the sense of a report from an interested party), as ***EMPRESA.1 was involved in the incident itself and, in fact, it partly arose from its failure to control and supervise the compliance by ***EMPRESA.9 with Endesa's instructions and its obligations as a data processor. It is clearly evident that it is a "defensive" report prepared by an entity that needs to protect its interests against potential liability claims, having acted as a data processor for Endesa and potentially bearing some degree of responsibility for the events that ultimately led to the present proceedings.  
Therefore, ENDESA understands that this is not an impartial expert or legal report on the incident prepared by an external party with no involvement in it and without interests in the outcome of this sanctioning file from which conclusions can be drawn to assess Endesa's compliance regarding: (i) the obligation to notify the incident to the AEPD; and (ii) the application of appropriate security measures.  
On the contrary, the purpose of this report is to shift all types of responsibility for the incident onto Endesa, attempting to make this Agency see that ***EMPRESA.1, even when it flagrantly breached its obligations as a data processor (did not provide adequate information to Endesa to advise on the scope and consequences of the incident, did not implement the necessary security measures...  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
64/214  
due to the risk and did not control compliance by ***COMPANY.9 with its obligations as a subcontractor), always acted with the utmost diligence and in strict compliance with the law (quad non).  
In this regard, this Agency wishes to point out that the actions of ***COMPANY.1 are not the subject of this procedure, but rather the actions of ENDESA in the violation of the security of the personal data reported.  
In this sense, as stated in all the documentation in the file and as detailed in the Background, Established Facts, and Legal Grounds of this document, this Agency is based not only on the report prepared by ***COMPANY.1 on November 4, 2021, but also on all the information it has become aware of throughout this procedure.  
Precisely due to its possible implication in the violation of the security of personal data, this Agency requested certain information from ***COMPANY.1, within the framework of the preliminary actions of this procedure. However, this is by no means the only information on which this Agency relies to propose the corresponding sanctions against ENDESA.  
For all the reasons stated, this allegation is dismissed.  
(b) Interactions with Facebook to request the removal of advertisements through which the sale/rental of access credentials to Endesa's systems was offered:  
ENDESA alleges that throughout the Initiation Agreement, the AEPD accuses it of a lack of diligence in requesting Facebook to remove those advertisements through which the sale/rental of access credentials to its systems was offered because: (i) it did not address Facebook Ireland Limited for this purpose, as Facebook Spain had proposed in response to the first burofax sent by Endesa on February 4, 2022; and (ii) it did not succeed in having Facebook remove said advertisements and, therefore, did not implement the proposed measures.  
ENDESA indicates that the Initiation Agreement errs in this interpretation since: (i) It is not true that Endesa did not send a communication to Facebook Ireland Limited due to a lack of diligence, but rather because it consciously, and after analyzing the implications of doing so, decided that doing so would precisely recognize Irish jurisdiction, which would imply, if necessary, having to direct any claim before the Irish courts, which was, and is, an inappropriate and unfair burden. This was the main reason why, instead, Endesa decided to send a second burofax to Facebook Spain on February 28, 2022 (which demonstrates, in its view, great diligence on the part of Endesa) insisting on its request).  
ENDESA states that recognizing Irish jurisdiction would imply assuming that all incidents occurring within the framework of the platform managed by Facebook would have a cross-border nature and, with that, any action would be debatable.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
65/214  
(legal or administrative) action that may be initiated against Facebook if not before the Irish authorities.  
(ii) It is not true that the fact that Facebook did not remove the ads through which the sale/rental of access credentials to Endesa's systems was offered is a consequence of a lack of diligence on the part of the Company, nor does it imply, as indicated in the Initiation Agreement, that the security measures proposed by Endesa at the beginning of the identification of the data security breach were not implemented.  
ENDESA states that: (a) Endesa sent numerous requests and even two burofaxes to Facebook Spain requesting the removal of the ads (Endesa cannot be held responsible for the non-removal of the ads since Facebook is solely responsible for the management of the platform and its publications); and (b) Endesa did implement the proposed security measures from the outset of the breach, which were adequate to mitigate the consequences of the Data Security Breach, since, even prior to sending the first burofax, it had already blocked access for the compromised users to Endesa's tools by disabling their credentials. In other words, the existence of obsolete credentials on Facebook was no longer relevant, as they no longer posed any risk to the rights of the affected parties.  
Once those credentials were disabled, and although the Facebook ads remained active, the risk from a data protection perspective was eliminated, since the possibility of accessing the data for which Endesa is responsible, stored in its tools, disappeared (even if the original credentials were sold/rented). Additionally, since Endesa proceeded to implement additional security measures such as multi-factor authentication and the deactivation of multi-session access, the possibility of unidentified users being able to gain unauthorized access to Endesa's systems was eliminated, regardless of whether new Facebook ads were published or the previous ones remained active.  
Notwithstanding the above, ENDESA explains that, since the fact that the ads remained active on Facebook could still imply the commission of an infringement or crime of disclosure of secrets or intellectual property, Endesa decided to insist on its claim against Facebook by sending a second burofax.  
For all these reasons, ENDESA emphasizes that, despite some Facebook ads remaining active—due to issues, in its opinion, unrelated to Endesa—the credentials offered through that medium no longer allowed access to systems ***HERRAMIENTA.1 and ***HERRAMIENTA.2 because they were disabled, and as a result of the new security measures implemented by Endesa, no additional unauthorized user would be able to achieve the purpose of selling/renting access credentials to Endesa's systems, as those measures would prevent it.  
However, ENDESA has commissioned an independent third party to prepare an expert report demonstrating the aforementioned aspects, which will be made available to the AEPD once completed.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6  
www.aepd.es  
28001 – Madrid  
sedeagpd.gob.es
66/214  
This report was submitted during the evidence practice period, through a document from ENDESA dated June 23, 2023, as Document number 1.  
In this regard, this Agency wishes to point out, first of all, that ENDESA cannot change the jurisdiction at will, nor can it decide who is responsible for the processing.  
In the present case, as indicated by Facebook Spain in its response to the burofax sent by ENDESA, the entity responsible for the processing of personal data at the European level is Facebook Ireland Limited, an entity incorporated in Ireland with its registered office in Dublin. As reported to ENDESA, Facebook Spain has no capacity to decide or take any action regarding what was requested by ENDESA in its burofax dated February 7, 2022. Therefore, ENDESA should have communicated with Facebook Ireland Limited, through the means provided by Facebook Spain (internet or postal).  
The definition of "main establishment" is set out in Article 4.16 of the GDPR. Regarding the data controllers, the aforementioned article states, in its letter a), that the main establishment is, "in the case of a data controller with establishments in more than one Member State, the place of its central administration in the Union, unless decisions regarding the purposes and means of processing are taken in another establishment of the controller in the Union and that latter establishment has the power to implement such decisions, in which case the establishment that has adopted such decisions shall be considered the main establishment." Therefore, in this case, the main establishment of Facebook in the Union is Facebook Ireland Limited, established in Ireland.  
Of course, all incidents that occur within the framework of the platform managed by Facebook have a cross-border nature, and for their management, the GDPR has enabled a "one-stop-shop mechanism" that allows interested parties in Spain to file a complaint regarding a matter concerning a company that has its main establishment in another Member State such as Ireland, which will be resolved jointly by the lead supervisory authority and the interested supervisory authorities (including the supervisory authority receiving the complaint), through the procedure provided for in Article 60 of the GDPR.  
So much so, that this Agency receives numerous complaints from interested parties in Spain against controllers with their main establishment in Ireland, who carry out cross-border processing of personal data and whose lead authority is the Irish Data Protection Authority. All this under the applicable regulations.  
For the reasons stated, this Agency reiterates that not having even attempted communication through the web with Facebook Ireland Limited to try to have the ads in question removed constitutes serious negligence on the part of ENDESA.  
Secondly, this Agency considers that ENDESA did not act with due diligence in preventing access to the systems by users whom ENDESA knew had been compromised.  
C/ Jorge Juan, 6
